::: {.report-title custom-style="Title"}
Protein consumption and alternative proteins in India and Pakistan
:::

Muhammad Umar Zafar

Evidence edition dated [13 October 2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="date"}

[AI-assisted]{.keep-word} research draft. Human scholarly review is pending. This report brings together the comparative brief, food and nutrition data, [consumer-study]{.keep-word} extraction, supply benchmark and [cultivated-chicken]{.keep-word} work. It distinguishes source observations, analyst calculations, documentary interpretation and unresolved questions. The accompanying edition presents the same evidence in a shorter visual sequence.

## The comparison is informative but an adoption baseline is still missing

India and Pakistan differ in the food available nationally, the quantities recorded by household surveys and the institutions relevant to new food products. These differences identify questions for a comparative study. They do not establish which country will adopt alternative proteins faster, how many consumers will buy them, or whether purchases will displace animal foods.

The principal findings are:

- Pakistan's poultry availability was [8.72]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_pakistan_poultry"} kg per person per year, compared with [3.52]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_india_poultry"} in India, in [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. These are FAOSTAT [food-balance]{.keep-word} quantities available for human consumption.
- India's pulse availability was [15.39]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_india_pulses"} kg per person per year, compared with [6.53]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_pakistan_pulses"} in Pakistan in the same year. Familiar plant foods therefore belong in a study of competing meals; pulse supply does not measure demand for a manufactured meat analogue.
- The State of Food Security and Nutrition in the World (SOFI) estimates that [40.4]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_india_unaffordable"} percent of India's population and [60.3]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_pakistan_unaffordable"} percent of Pakistan's could not afford a healthy diet in [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Economic access is a separate outcome from measured dietary intake.
- The selected Joint Child Malnutrition Estimates (JME) country model estimates [under-five]{.keep-word} stunting at [32.9]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_india_stunting"} percent in India and [33.6]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_pakistan_stunting"} percent in Pakistan for [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. These modelled estimates cannot be added to unaffordability or undernourishment counts.
- India's official [household-based]{.keep-word} protein estimates can be compared with adult requirement scenarios only after retaining the published [consumer-unit]{.keep-word} denominator. The consumer unit is based on energy requirements, so this comparison cannot establish protein adequacy or population deficiency.
- Pakistan's previously displayed complete protein total is withdrawn. Named physical conversions now describe a partial food basket, with unresolved foods and denominator reconciliation left visible. No national adequacy estimate or rural and urban model ranking survives that repair.
- The supply benchmark predicts national protein availability under exploratory models. Its [held-out]{.keep-word} interval results differ sharply between the country series, and its forecast intervals do not supply probabilities of [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} adoption.
- The [consumer-study]{.keep-word} register contains [9]{.claim data-claim="package_p07_study_records"} studies and [29]{.claim data-claim="package_p07_finding_records"} extracted findings. These are heterogeneous published results, not respondent microdata or a bilateral acceptance estimate.
- India has documented research support and [food-regulatory]{.keep-word} instruments; Pakistan has halal institutions and a Punjab instrument containing a general [novel-food]{.keep-word} provision. Neither a programme nor a rule establishes approval or sales of a named cultivated product.
- The halal work identifies product, process and institutional conditions. A religious position, a halal certificate and food authorization must each be established for the case being assessed.

The main unresolved empirical requirement is a compatible observation of consumer behaviour for a specified product, place and period. The current record does not provide that baseline. The most useful next work is to obtain eligible respondent data, verify actual product availability and test questions about price, meal use and repeat purchase before making an adoption forecast.

## A common meal makes the comparative question testable

::: {data-package="P01"}

The India–Pakistan brief asks when a new protein product earns a recurring place in a household meal. It distinguishes willingness to try from purchase, habitual use and the food displaced. That distinction remains the report's organizing question. National food supply describes the context in which people choose; it does not observe their response to a new product. The brief is a secondary synthesis, with no project consumer experiment or interview evidence.

The primary proposed comparison is a [plant-based]{.keep-word} chicken product, a conventional chicken option and a familiar [pulse-based]{.keep-word} meal. The options need specified serving sizes, prices, preparation demands and product descriptions. A [cultivated-meat]{.keep-word} vignette would be analysed separately because its animal origin and production process introduce different questions. [Precision-fermented]{.keep-word} ingredients and fungal biomass also require their own descriptions. A response to one category cannot stand for acceptance of all alternative proteins.

The brief's argument has an economic rival: price, taste and availability may account for differences that appear cultural. Comparable purchase conditions might reveal that differences within a country exceed those between selected groups in the two countries. Religious affiliation, urban residence or an aggregate food quantity cannot settle that question. The India–Pakistan research brief proposes a comparison that can distinguish these rival explanations.

:::

::: {data-package="P02"}

The foundational plan established an India–Pakistan research lane alongside support for [forecast-question]{.keep-word} design. Its early [policy-attention]{.keep-word} hypothesis was a proposal to investigate, not an empirical result. The later halal work expands the documented scope without cancelling the original comparative question. This report connects the workstreams through [product-specific]{.keep-word} evidence while retaining their different methods.

A forecast question needs a named product category, geography, outcome, denominator, deadline and resolution source. Retail availability, repeated purchasing and [animal-food]{.keep-word} displacement are different events. Where no compatible baseline or resolution source exists, the question remains a monitoring or [data-acquisition]{.keep-word} task. Repeated model answers do not supply the missing observation, and disagreement between language models is not a calibrated probability interval.

:::

## The drivers are hypotheses about decisions

Affordability concerns what a household can buy relative to familiar meals. The relevant comparison is a delivered serving at an actual outlet, including preparation and access costs where measured. A laboratory cost estimate or premium retail offer cannot establish a typical household's choice. The household expenditure gradients in this report motivate [price-sensitive]{.keep-word} questions without identifying an elasticity for alternative proteins.

Dietary practice concerns ingredients, meal roles, preparation and the people who buy and cook. A product resembling meat may appeal to someone seeking a substitute and repel someone avoiding [meat-like]{.keep-word} foods. Both possibilities fit the present evidence. A questionnaire should therefore ask what the proposed product would replace and whether it fits an ordinary meal, rather than treating resemblance as a universal advantage.

Social legitimacy concerns whose approval matters and how a choice is understood in the household or community. The report's culture matrix contains descriptive context, not a causal ranking of religions or nationalities. Region, income, caste, gender, access and dietary practice can overlap. Their independent contributions require a design that measures those relationships instead of assigning them from a national label.

Certification concerns the credibility and scope of an assurance for a named product. A consumer's belief, a religious authority's conditions and a certifier's decision are separate observations. For cultivated meat, donor history and culture inputs may matter as much as the final label. A generic assertion that a product is [animal-free]{.keep-word} cannot resolve questions about every earlier production stage.

Public policy concerns permission to sell, research support, standards, disclosure and the institutions responsible for those decisions. The plausible connection to consumption is through products, prices, access or trust. The current documentary evidence records instruments and events; it does not identify their effects on purchases. A funded project remains distinct from a commercial product reaching a household.

The proposed sequence from acceptability to trial, purchase and habit is an analytical framework. It is not an estimated causal model. Trial could replace meat, replace a familiar plant dish or add an occasional novelty. Repeated purchases and the displaced food would need to be observed before the study could assess substitution.

## Different data describe different parts of the food system

::: {data-package="P03"}

The protein dashboard supplies the national, regional, dietary, nutrition, company, policy and cultural tables used here. Its repaired datasets preserve source identifiers, units, vintages and [row-level]{.keep-word} evidence tiers. A source ledger describes the publication or data source; a raw manifest records downloaded bytes and retrieval dates. A successful download establishes retrieval, while a source locator and compatible interpretation establish what a row can support.

The report uses official food balances for national availability, household surveys for their stated [household-based]{.keep-word} measures and nutrition indicators for their own populations and estimators. Analyst calculations carry a derived tag. A calculation based on an official table does not inherit the table's observational status. Missing values remain blank, and the edition avoids drawing lines through them as if they were observed zeros.

Every displayed quantitative claim has a ledger entry specifying a dataset, exact filter, operation, unit, source identifiers, locator and rounding rule. The verification program recomputes values from the selected rows. It also checks visible numbers, chart data, source joins, package coverage, review evidence, browser receipts, prose, public links, publication files, the library build and export text. Deliberate corruptions tested these checks before the edition and report were built. The verification record describes what those checks actually establish; it does not confer scholarly approval.

AI agents collected and processed sources under the author's direction, assembled the tables and drafted this synthesis. The source records retain access limits and unresolved interpretations. The agents did not conduct a consumer survey, obtain project interview evidence, certify a product or establish a causal effect. Human scholarly review remains pending.

:::

::: {data-package="P05"}

The earlier data assessment is a dated scoping document. It reviewed a selected set of files and identified useful [food-balance]{.keep-word} material, gaps in data access and possible policy distinctions. Subsequent source repair changes how some of its statements can be used. Its description of an older unopened halal register does not describe the separately audited paper register, and its Pakistan [policy-absence]{.keep-word} language is superseded by the Punjab evidence discussed below.

The assessment is preserved rather than rewritten retrospectively. Its contribution is to the history of the research question and the identification of inputs. Current numerical findings come from the repaired tables and claim ledger. A historical assessment, a later source audit and independent human verification have different evidential standing.

:::

::: {data-package="P12"}

The approved design preview supplied the editorial visual direction: readable prose, separate country colours, source lines and direct access to tables. It was a precursor to the full edition. Its old figures and browser receipts are historical, so they cannot validate the repaired page. The final edition reads the current processed data and binds its browser checks to the actual HTML bytes.

Regional maps retain the selected boundary source and its original metadata. Jammu and Kashmir, Ladakh, Azad Kashmir and [Gilgit-Baltistan]{.keep-word} are hatched without a data colour; values remain available in the corresponding source tables where present. This treatment prevents the colour scale from implying a territorial determination. It does not reconcile differences between survey geography and the boundary edition. Narrow screens use ranked displays where a map would obscure the evidence.

:::

## Pakistan has more poultry availability while India has more pulses

FAOSTAT records [8.72]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_pakistan_poultry"} kg of poultry available per person per year in Pakistan and [3.52]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_india_poultry"} in India in [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. For pulses, the order reverses: [15.39]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_india_pulses"} kg in India and [6.53]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_pakistan_pulses"} in Pakistan. Milk excluding butter is [87.43]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_india_milk"} kg in India and [129.71]{.claim data-claim="fbs_2023_pakistan_milk"} in Pakistan. The source is the saved FAOSTAT [food-balance]{.keep-word} release, with its flags and commodity definitions retained. [FAOSTAT food balances](<https://www.fao.org/faostat/en/#data/FBS>)

Food balance sheets estimate food available for human consumption within a national accounting system. They do not measure an individual's ingestion, household distribution or retail sales of alternative proteins. The series can compare recorded availability under the source's definitions. It cannot identify who consumed the food, how much was wasted after supply entered households or which products competed at a particular meal.

The pulse comparison matters because familiar plant foods are already part of ordinary food systems. Large pulse availability may be compatible with interest in a meat analogue, indifference to it or preference for a familiar dish. None of those responses follows from supply alone. A study that compares an analogue only with meat would miss a relevant existing option for some consumers.

The OECD–FAO Agricultural Outlook uses a separate method and weight convention. Its poultry projection is shown apart from the historical [food-balance]{.keep-word} series, with its vintage and [retail-weight]{.keep-word} unit. Joining the projected value directly to the FAOSTAT endpoint would create an apparent change partly from incompatible definitions. The report treats the outlook as a conditional projection from its source rather than an extension of measured household consumption. [OECD–FAO Agricultural Outlook](<https://sdmx.oecd.org/public/rest/data/OECD.TAD.ATM,DSD_AGR@DF_OUTLOOK_2025_2034,1.1/IND+PAK.A....?startPeriod=2010&amp;endPeriod=2034&amp;dimensionAtObservation=AllDimensions>)

For [2034]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, that outlook projects poultry consumption at [2.30]{.claim data-claim="oecd_2034_india_poultry_meat_oecd_human_consumption_projection"} kg per person per year in India and [5.70]{.claim data-claim="oecd_2034_pakistan_poultry_meat_oecd_human_consumption_projection"} in Pakistan, in retail weight. The source release covers [2025–2034]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. The numerical difference from the [food-balance]{.keep-word} endpoint is not an estimated decline under a common measurement system.

## Regional food frequency and household quantities answer different questions

India's National Family Health Survey (NFHS-5) measures reported food frequency among specified respondent groups. Its fish, chicken or meat category combines foods and records frequency rather than portion size. The regional panels retain the sex, age group, residence, fieldwork period and tabulation base applicable to each row. A [weekly-frequency]{.keep-word} percentage cannot be converted to kilograms or grams of protein without information the table does not provide. [National Family Health Survey](<https://dhsprogram.com/pubs/pdf/FR375/FR375.pdf>)

Pakistan's Household Integrated Economic Survey, HIES, supplies [household-based]{.keep-word} quantities and expenditure measures. The revised quantity workbook reports monthly [per-capita]{.keep-word} quantities, so calling them household totals would change their meaning. Its regional chicken rows can describe differences within that survey's design. They cannot be placed on the same colour scale as India's reported weekly food frequency and interpreted as a bilateral dietary ranking. [HIES revised quantity table](<https://www.pbs.gov.pk/wp-content/uploads/2020/07/TABLE_23-2.xls>)

The Pakistan National Nutrition Survey contributes separate nutrition context. Its population and indicators differ from HIES food quantities. The maps and tables retain geographic coverage gaps and distinguish the former merged areas where the source requires it. A missing join to the chosen boundary file is an unresolved mapping problem, not a zero observation.

Residence differences in these tables are descriptive. Urban and rural respondents or households differ in many measured and unmeasured circumstances. Neither the map nor the residence toggle identifies an effect of urbanisation. The useful [follow-up]{.keep-word} is to examine access, prices, expenditure and meal practices in specified locations before selecting study sites.

## Food budgets and protein shares show different dietary structures

Pakistan's chicken quantity rises across the selected expenditure quintiles in the revised HIES table. This is a [per-capita]{.keep-word} quantity gradient within one source, not a measured preference for an alternative product. Household size also differs across these groups, which makes denominator discipline necessary. A household total and a [per-capita]{.keep-word} quantity can order groups differently without either describing the same estimand.

The lowest quintile records [0.17]{.claim data-claim="hies_chicken_meat_q1"} kg of chicken per person per month, compared with [0.65]{.claim data-claim="hies_chicken_meat_q5"} in the highest. The accompanying [household-size]{.keep-word} context falls from [7.56]{.claim data-claim="hies_household_size_q1"} to [4.65]{.claim data-claim="hies_household_size_q5"} persons across those groups. The quantities come from the revised HIES [2024–25]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} workbook, with the precise cells recorded in the claims ledger. The exact analytical base for that revised table is not established by the report's broader [household-count]{.keep-word} context.

India's Household Consumption Expenditure Survey, HCES, converts reported household food into nutrient estimates and reports protein contributions by food group. Cereals, pulses, dairy and the combined eggs, fish and meat group each have a distinct role. The combined [animal-food]{.keep-word} group in that table excludes dairy. Its share cannot be described as all [animal-source]{.keep-word} protein. [Nutritional Intake in India](<https://www.mospi.gov.in/sites/default/files/publication_reports/Nutritional_Intake_in_India_L.pdf>)

The Indian fractile labels rank the population by monthly [per-capita]{.keep-word} consumption expenditure (MPCE), from lower to higher spending. Rural and urban populations are ranked separately. The labels describe positions in those expenditure distributions; they are not percentages below a protein requirement or shares of the food budget.

These tables broaden the comparative question beyond replacing meat. A product might compete with a cereal accompaniment, a pulse dish, dairy or a convenience meal. The report cannot determine that competition from ingredient composition. It requires asking consumers about a specified occasion and recording the food actually replaced.

Expenditure measures need equal care. A [food-budget]{.keep-word} share is a proportion of food expenditure, not total household expenditure, energy or protein. A quantity gradient is not a price elasticity. The source rows retain those distinctions so that apparent agreement between indicators does not create an unsupported common scale.

## The Indian benchmark is a scenario and the Pakistan total is missing

India's published [consumer-unit]{.keep-word} estimates are the appropriate denominator for the report's adult reference scenarios. A consumer unit expresses energy needs relative to a sedentary adult man. It is not a [protein-equivalent]{.keep-word} person. Comparing a household group's mean protein estimate with an adult requirement therefore supplies a descriptive scenario, with an explicit mismatch that prevents an adequacy conclusion.

The [all-India]{.keep-word} estimates are [66.6]{.claim data-claim="india_cu_rural_mpce_all_india"} g per consumer unit per day in rural areas and [69.9]{.claim data-claim="india_cu_urban_mpce_all_india"} in urban areas. HCES fieldwork ran from August [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} through July [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}; these months describe the survey period, not an individual respondent's observation interval. The nutrient table's analytical base remains distinct from the survey's overall household sample.

The Estimated Average Requirement, EAR, is the primary reference; the Recommended Dietary Allowance, RDA, is retained as a labelled sensitivity. They answer different questions about requirements and cannot be swapped merely to produce a larger apparent gap. The report also retains alternative [body-weight]{.keep-word} assumptions. None of these comparisons observes the distribution of usual individual intake needed for an estimate of population inadequacy.

At a reference weight of [60]{.claim data-claim="requirement_india_ear_60kg_weight"} kg, the EAR scenario uses [0.66]{.claim data-claim="requirement_india_ear_60kg_coefficient"} g per kg per day, or [39.60]{.claim data-claim="requirement_india_ear_60kg"} g per day. The RDA sensitivity uses [0.83]{.claim data-claim="requirement_india_rda_60kg_coefficient"} g per kg per day, or [49.80]{.claim data-claim="requirement_india_rda_60kg"} g per day. These calculations are tagged derived even though their requirement coefficients come from reference sources. They are adult scenarios without a [protein-equivalent]{.keep-word} household denominator.

A group mean above a reference can coexist with individuals below it. A group mean below a reference does not tell how many individuals are below it. Counting expenditure groups is especially misleading when those groups differ in population or household composition. The former unweighted fraction of groups below a benchmark has therefore been removed rather than described as a population deficiency rate.

Pakistan's former [full-diet]{.keep-word} estimate used a [pooled-basket]{.keep-word} cost calibration that did not establish complete protein intake. The current model withdraws that total and preserves it only as historical output. The replacement converts selected reported food quantities through named [food-composition]{.keep-word} factors and [edible-portion]{.keep-word} assumptions. It reports a [matched-food]{.keep-word} subtotal and a separate subtotal for chicken, beef, mutton, eggs and fish.

The composition evidence comes from the [Pakistan Food Composition Table](<https://www.fao.org/fileadmin/templates/food_composition/documents/regional/Book_Food_Composition_Table_for_Pakistan_.pdf>), [Food Composition Table for Bangladesh](<https://www.fao.org/fileadmin/templates/food_composition/documents/FCT_10_2_14_final_version.pdf>), [Indian Food Composition Tables](<https://www.nin.res.in/ebooks/IFCT2017.pdf>) and [USDA SR Legacy release](<https://fdc.nal.usda.gov/fdc-datasets/FoodData_Central_sr_legacy_food_csv_2018-04.zip>). [Pakistan-table]{.keep-word} food entries anchor the local matches; the Bangladesh Rohu entry supplies distinct [edible-portion]{.keep-word} assumptions, while Indian and USDA entries supply explicitly named [food-form]{.keep-word} alternatives. The [INFOODS density database](<https://www.fao.org/fileadmin/templates/food_composition/documents/density_DB_v2_0_01.pdf>) supports [volume-to-mass]{.keep-word} assumptions. [USDA documentation](<https://www.ars.usda.gov/arsuserfiles/80400525/data/sr-legacy/sr-legacy_doc.pdf>) defines the [edible-portion]{.keep-word} basis; its portion weights do not establish Pakistani [egg-size]{.keep-word} frequencies. The model also treats [tea-transfer]{.keep-word} and [product-mixture]{.keep-word} choices as conditional assumptions. These sources provide composition and method inputs, not measured Pakistani intake.

For each named endpoint, the [[scenario-factor]{.keep-word} table](../methods/factor_evidence.csv) identifies the conditional food conversions. Its factor identifiers join the [[source-factor]{.keep-word} catalogue](../methods/source_factor_catalog.csv), which gives original PDF pages, food codes, units and [source-file]{.keep-word} hashes. The claims ledger preserves that join and the complete [composition-source]{.keep-word} list alongside the household quantity cells. The [method guide](../methods/README.md) explains how to follow this record without treating an assumed food match as an observed product mix.

The national [matched-food]{.keep-word} subtotal is [39.27]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_partial_all_all_matched_basket_subtotal_low"} to [40.61]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_partial_all_all_matched_basket_subtotal_high"} g per person per day. The separate chicken, beef, mutton, eggs and fish subtotal is [3.30]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_partial_all_all_five_animal_foods_subtotal_low"} to [4.64]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_partial_all_all_five_animal_foods_subtotal_high"} g per person per day. The low method is `bd_cuts_rohu_us_small`; the high method is `edible_flesh_us_jumbo`. Their differences concern edible portions, fish matching and [egg-size]{.keep-word} assumptions, rather than sampled uncertainty.

Of [38]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_model_n_table23_foods"} food rows, [27]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_model_n_converted_foods"} receive a conditional conversion; [0]{.claim data-claim="pakistan_model_imputed_food_count"} are imputed. The separate [animal-food]{.keep-word} subtotal excludes dairy and other [animal-source]{.keep-word} foods. Neither subtotal is called total protein intake, and no quintile ordering is inferred from these incomplete sums.

The method range expresses the extrema among named conditional conversions. It is not a sampling confidence interval or a complete uncertainty bound. Unresolved foods, [prepared-food]{.keep-word} coverage, [food-form]{.keep-word} mixtures and quantity reconciliation remain outside the subtotal. No imputation is used to silently complete the diet. The range therefore remains separate from the Indian [consumer-unit]{.keep-word} panel and from the adult EAR/RDA scenarios.

The unresolved food quantities and local product mixtures are the next inputs needed before a complete Pakistani intake estimate can be attempted.

## Affordability and child growth reveal overlapping concerns

SOFI estimates that [40.4]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_india_unaffordable"} percent of India's population and [60.3]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_pakistan_unaffordable"} percent of Pakistan's could not afford a healthy diet in [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. The measure combines an [economic-access]{.keep-word} model with a specified [healthy-diet]{.keep-word} cost. It does not observe every household's actual food purchases or diagnose a nutrient deficiency. The selected source vintage remains attached to the figures. [The State of Food Security and Nutrition in the World](<https://www.fao.org/fileadmin/templates/cfs/CFS53/Plenary_documents/Inf.15_SOFI_Report/EN_compiled.pdf>)

The JME country model estimates [under-five]{.keep-word} stunting at [32.9]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_india_stunting"} percent and [33.6]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_pakistan_stunting"} percent respectively for [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. The [country-model]{.keep-word} release and survey observations appear separately because a modelled annual estimate is not a new household measurement. The model rows retain uncertainty intervals and provenance where supplied. [Joint Child Malnutrition Estimates country model](<https://cdn.who.int/media/docs/default-source/child-growth/child-growth-standards/jme-country-estimates-march2026.xlsx?sfvrsn=65301f6b_3>)

Stunting reflects accumulated growth constraints with multiple causes. It cannot be used as a [protein-deficiency]{.keep-word} measure or as an addressable market for a meat substitute. Undernourishment likewise uses its own definition, population and estimation procedure. The same person can fall within several indicators, and their reference periods can differ. Adding their counts would [double-count]{.keep-word} people and combine incompatible outcomes.

The report therefore presents an explicit instruction not to add these indicators. Their joint relevance is to research design: affordability, dietary quality and child growth deserve attention, but each needs an appropriate intervention question. The current evidence does not establish that a particular [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} product is affordable, suitable for the affected population or effective against any of these outcomes.

## Exploratory supply forecasts need better calibration evidence

::: {data-package="P08"}

The standalone benchmark models annual national protein supply using frozen historical series. Total and [animal-source]{.keep-word} protein are separate series for each country. Its measured outcome is available supply, not [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} purchases, consumption frequency or an adoption share. The selected forecast horizon extends to [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, subject to the benchmark's assumptions and short observed history.

The frozen input contains [56]{.claim data-claim="package_p08_annual_observations"} annual observations and the selected output contains [48]{.claim data-claim="package_p08_selected_forecasts"} forecast records. The protocol defaults to persistence because its [model-selection]{.keep-word} evidence is too short to support a more ambitious selection claim. Consequently, the selected point forecasts retain the last observed level while uncertainty grows with the horizon. A flat point path is a stated benchmark assumption, not evidence that supply will be constant.

Selected persistence forecasts for [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, grams per person per day. The lower and upper bounds are the benchmark's nominal [80]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_nominal_level"} percent interval, with the coverage limitations below.

| Country | Protein supply | Point | Lower | Upper |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| India | [Animal-source]{.keep-word} | [20.01]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_animal_forecast_2035"} | [16.67]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_animal_lower80_2035"} | [23.35]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_animal_upper80_2035"} |
| India | Total | [74.57]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_forecast_2035"} | [68.14]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_lower80_2035"} | [81.00]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_upper80_2035"} |
| Pakistan | [Animal-source]{.keep-word} | [32.55]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_animal_forecast_2035"} | [28.50]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_animal_lower80_2035"} | [36.60]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_animal_upper80_2035"} |
| Pakistan | Total | [73.39]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_total_forecast_2035"} | [65.23]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_total_lower80_2035"} | [81.55]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_total_upper80_2035"} |

Source: [Protein-supply]{.keep-word} benchmark. All forecasts are derived.

At the nominal [80]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_nominal_level"} percent interval level, the selected [total-supply]{.keep-word} interval covers [1]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_coverage_count"} of [6]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_total_evaluation_count"} [held-out]{.keep-word} Indian cells and [6]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_total_coverage_count"} of [6]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_total_evaluation_count"} Pakistani cells. The [animal-source]{.keep-word} series has the same [country-specific]{.keep-word} coverage counts: [1]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_animal_coverage_count"} of [6]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_india_animal_evaluation_count"} for India and [6]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_animal_coverage_count"} of [6]{.claim data-claim="benchmark_pakistan_animal_evaluation_count"} for Pakistan. The evaluation rows and split remain attached to these results.

The [held-out]{.keep-word} comparison reports very different interval coverage across countries. The report retains the actual counts and evaluation denominator alongside the nominal interval level. A small set of overlapping evaluation cells cannot demonstrate stable calibration. Broad intervals may cover observations without producing useful precision, while narrow intervals can miss them repeatedly. Neither result supports interpreting the forecast as a calibrated statement about future consumers.

The uneven [held-out]{.keep-word} coverage is a reason to investigate the interval method before using these supply scenarios for decisions. Neither the calibration exercise nor its forecast contains a measured [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} market share.

The missing adoption baseline cannot be supplied by relabelling this model. A future adoption study would need observations of the relevant product and outcome, a compatible denominator and a resolution source. Until those inputs exist, the supply benchmark remains a separate research product.

The package also contains statistical diagnostics, a hypothesised causal graph and a policy calculator with assumed pilot budgets. These describe proposed relationships or [analyst-selected]{.keep-word} scenarios. Installed [causal-analysis]{.keep-word} software does not identify an effect, and a calculator's arithmetic does not validate its national parameters. The evidence index supports source retrieval without generating a new consumer observation.

:::

## Industry records establish events rather than a market total

The company register distinguishes a business, a product category, a dated event and its evidence. A directory listing is not proof that a factory operates at its stated capacity. A company website can document a claim or offer without independently confirming sales. Closures and exclusions remain visible so that an earlier optimistic record is not silently carried forward as current operation.

UnCrave's reported closure is recorded as a dated event. The announcement date does not establish the exact date on which every product ceased to be available. The Phyx company lead is recorded as an India lead rather than a Pakistan company. The food/feed rule excludes [feed-only]{.keep-word} activity from [human-food]{.keep-word} company totals and retains adjacent or [mixed-use]{.keep-word} businesses with explicit category limits. A traditional [soy-food]{.keep-word} or [plant-milk]{.keep-word} business does not become a [meat-alternative]{.keep-word} producer merely because its inputs are [plant-derived]{.keep-word}.

Pakistan's identified retail material is similarly bounded. A catalogue or [import-to-order]{.keep-word} offer demonstrates that a seller presented a product under particular terms. It does not prove current stock, completed sales, nationwide availability or repeat purchasing. Dated secondary reporting on a business remains reporting from that date unless a newer operational source is recovered.

Pulse production provides potential feedstock context, with crop definitions and source flags retained. [Whole-crop]{.keep-word} tonnage can have several end uses and cannot be converted into [alternative-meat]{.keep-word} output, isolate capacity or sales without additional process and allocation evidence. Aggregates and constituent crops also overlap, so they are not added as independent supplies.

The bounded [public-source]{.keep-word} search recovered newer industry, [consumer-awareness]{.keep-word}, export and [global-market]{.keep-word} material. It did not establish a compatible newer estimate of India's domestic [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} market. This is a narrower conclusion than saying no newer GFI information exists. Historical national scenarios, global sales and export opportunities remain different quantities.

### [Job-board]{.keep-word} records widen the Indian company list

AltProtein.Jobs reviewed an earlier draft and supplied two tables. One is a directory of companies featured on its job board; the other counts vacancies by country and year. Both describe recruitment on one platform. Neither measures production, sales or national employment.

The directory has [119]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_india_rows"} rows for India, entered as either India or IN. They cover [115]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_india_names"} distinct company names after trimming and lowercasing; a few [near-duplicates]{.keep-word}, such as two Shaka Harry entries, remain. The report's own register holds [12]{.claim data-claim="companies_register_india_count"} Indian records. By manual name matching, nine of those twelve also appear in the directory. The directory is therefore a much larger list of Indian candidates for verification. The supplier describes it as dated to [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and not fully current, so a listing does not show that a company operates today.

Its posting column counts job posts since October [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and records no end date. Indian companies hold [102]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_india_postings"} of those posts, spread across [15]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_india_posting_names"} companies; one further Indian row has no posting value. Tata Consumer Products accounts for [64]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_india_tata_consumer_products"} posts. Tata is a diversified food company, so its posts cannot all be assigned to [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} work.

### India and Brazil lead the Global South job market on this platform

According to AltProtein.Jobs, the vacancy table underlies its [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} job market report ([report page](<https://altprotein.jobs/career-hiring-report/>)) and covers [76]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_country_count"} countries. India recorded [381]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_india_2023"}, [490]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_india_2024"} and [529]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_india_2025"} vacancies in [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Brazil recorded [152]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_brazil_2023"}, [499]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_brazil_2024"} and [618]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_brazil_2025"}. India led Brazil in [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}; Brazil led in the two later years.

In [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, only the United States, Germany, France, the United Kingdom and the Netherlands recorded more vacancies than Brazil and India. Every country above them is in North America or Europe. On this platform, India and Brazil are therefore the two largest job markets outside those regions. The ranking depends on which countries are counted as Global South, and India is not the sole leader in either later year.

India's growth was slower than the platform's. The table total rose from [12111]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_total_2024"} to [15354]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_total_2025"} between [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, so India's share of all recorded vacancies fell even as its count rose.

### Pakistan appears in both tables with different pictures

The directory holds [2]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_pakistan_rows"} Pakistan rows: Shafi Gluco Chem and Shafi Gluco Chem (Pvt) Limited, probably one company under two names. Both rows record [0]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_pakistan_postings"} posts since October [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. They disagree on business model: one lists ingredients and inputs, the other [end-consumer]{.keep-word} brands. None of the register's [4]{.claim data-claim="companies_register_pakistan_count"} Pakistani companies appears in the directory.

The vacancy table nevertheless records [33]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_pakistan_2023"}, [44]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_pakistan_2024"} and [58]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_pakistan_2025"} vacancies for Pakistan in [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Neither table states whether country means company headquarters or job location. [Pakistan-based]{.keep-word} roles at companies listed elsewhere could explain the difference, but the tables cannot confirm it. Both observations are kept rather than reconciled by assumption.

### A commercial Asia forecast publishes growth bands without its inputs

AltProtein.Jobs also suggested the IndexBox report on the Asian [plant-based]{.keep-word} market, which forecasts to [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. The free report page ([IndexBox Asia [plant-based]{.keep-word} portfolio](<https://www.indexbox.io/store/asia-plant-based-portfolio-market-analysis-forecast-size-trends-and-insights/>)) gives regional growth bands for [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} to [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. It publishes no absolute market size, [base-year]{.keep-word} value or country figure, and no separate South Asia or Pakistan estimate. India appears only in qualitative statements about price sensitivity, foodservice and labelling rules.

The page describes its method as category reconstruction from company disclosures, retailer and [e-commerce]{.keep-word} checks, pricing review and triangulation. It names no proxy variable, coefficient or input value. Its scope excludes cultivated meat, bulk ingredients, protein powders and [foodservice-only]{.keep-word} items. The publisher's general methodology adds [AI-based]{.keep-word} normalisation and imputation ([IndexBox methodology](<https://app.indexbox.io/meta/>)).

Two features weaken the page as a benchmark. Its regional growth band is identical to the band on the IndexBox page for Pakistani synthetic protein, which covers a different product scope ([IndexBox Pakistan synthetic protein](<https://www.indexbox.io/store/pakistan-synthetic-protein-market-analysis-forecast-size-trends-and-insights/>)). Its list of country roles names the United States, the United Kingdom, Germany and Brazil, none of them in Asia. Both suggest templated text. The report therefore holds the IndexBox bands under the same rule as other figures whose [AI-assisted]{.keep-word} derivation cannot be checked; the companion data record them with locators. For this project's own forecast, the page shows what a usable benchmark must state: a baseline value, a unit, a product scope and the proxies that carry it forward.

## A speculative proxy estimate places both markets far below the US per person

The project team agreed to answer one question three ways: how large the [alternative-protein]{.keep-word} market will be in [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. The US section uses [language-model]{.keep-word} forecasts. This section gives the answer for India and Pakistan from proxy measures compared with the US. Every value below is a speculative scenario. None is an observed market size, an adoption rate or a forecast with tested calibration. The scenarios do not fill the missing adoption baseline described elsewhere in this report.

The calculation was set up during an [AI-assisted]{.keep-word} session and awaits human review. Its parameter bounds come from the cited sources, except two analyst assumptions: the lower bound of the income elasticity and the range of weights on [animal-source]{.keep-word} protein. The choice of proxies, the functional form and the uniform distributions are also analyst choices.

### The team has not yet fixed four definitions

The team's agreed question names years but not the market's unit. The defaults below are proposals for the author and team to confirm. Changing any of them changes the numbers.

| Field and proposed default |
| --- |
| Currency: US dollars at market exchange rates. Reason: the US comparison is in dollars, and market size means spending, not purchasing power. Effect of a different choice: [purchasing-power]{.keep-word} dollars would raise both countries' values because local prices are lower than US prices. |
| Price year: Constant [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} prices. Reason: the latest US observation is for [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Effect of a different choice: a nominal series would add inflation, which differs between the countries. |
| Value basis: Retail (consumer) sales. Reason: the US anchor is retail scan data. Effect of a different choice: producer or [ex-factory]{.keep-word} value would be lower by the distribution margin. |
| Product scope: [Plant-based]{.keep-word} meat, dairy and egg alternatives and [fermentation-derived]{.keep-word} foods sold for domestic human consumption. Reason: matches the GFI India domestic estimate. Effect of a different choice: adding cultivated meat changes nothing before approval; adding exports or ingredients changes the quantity measured. |

Two mismatches remain under these defaults. The US figure covers every [plant-based]{.keep-word} category tracked by SPINS, including prepared meals. The GFI India source does not state whether its estimate is retail value or in nominal rupees; this section assumes nominal [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} rupees at retail value.

### One Indian observation is carried forward by proxies

The only domestic market observation for either country is the GFI India estimate of [351]{.claim data-claim="proxyin_gfi_india_domestic_2022_central"} crore rupees for [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} ([GFI India smart protein economic analysis](<https://gfi-india.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/06/GFI-India-economic-analysis.pdf>)). Inflated with IMF consumer prices and converted at the [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} exchange rate, it is [45]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2022_anchor_market_value_value"} million dollars, or [0.03]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2022_anchor_per_capita_value"} dollars per person. US [plant-based]{.keep-word} retail sales were [7.9]{.claim data-claim="proxyin_gfi_us_retail_sales_2025"} billion dollars in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} ([GFI US retail market data](<https://gfi.org/marketresearch/>)), or [23.11]{.claim data-claim="proxy_united_states_2025_retail_per_capita_value"} dollars per person. US spending per person was therefore about [727]{.claim data-claim="proxy_united_states_2025_per_capita_ratio_to_india_anchor_value"} times India's, while US income per person was about [38.1]{.claim data-claim="proxy_united_states_2025_market_income_ratio_to_india_anchor_value"} times India's. Both ratios are in dollars at market exchange rates and [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} prices; India's [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} income per person is converted in the same way as the anchor ([World Bank GDP per person](<https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/IND;PAK;USA/indicator/NY.GDP.PCAP.CD?format=json&amp;per_page=200&amp;date=2020:2025>)).

India's value per person in a later year equals the anchor multiplied by three factors. The first is growth in real income per person, raised to an income elasticity. The second is the change in the urban share of the population. The third is a yearly drift that stands for everything income and urbanisation do not capture, including price gaps, taste and product availability. Pakistan's value per person equals India's in the same year, adjusted for relative income per person, relative urban share, relative [animal-source]{.keep-word} protein supply and relative industry activity. Totals multiply value per person by IMF population.

| Proxy, role and values used |
| --- |
| Income per person: carries spending forward and scales Pakistan to India. Real income per person in [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} is [2.17]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_income_index_from_2022_value"} times its [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} level in India and [1.25]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_income_index_from_2022_value"} times in Pakistan; Pakistan's [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} level is [0.59]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2025_market_income_ratio_to_india_value"} of India's in current US dollars. Source: [IMF World Economic Outlook](<https://www.imf.org/external/datamapper/api/v1/NGDP_RPCH>) for growth; [World Bank GDP per person](<https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/IND;PAK;USA/indicator/NY.GDP.PCAP.CD?format=json&amp;per_page=200&amp;date=2020:2025>) for the levels. IMF projections end in [2031]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and the [2031]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} growth rate is held to [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. |
| Income elasticity: how strongly spending follows income. Drawn between [1]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2022_income_elasticity_low"} (spending share constant) and [1.81]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2022_income_elasticity_high"} (income alone explains the whole gap between US and Indian spending, with spending and income both in market dollars). Source: Upper bound calculated from the US and India anchors and World Bank GDP per person. |
| Urban share: packaged products sell mainly through urban retail. India [35.7]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2025_urban_share_value"} percent in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [38.8]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_urban_share_value"} in [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}; Pakistan [39.5]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2025_urban_share_value"} and [43.3]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_urban_share_value"}. Source: [World Bank urban population share](<https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/IND;PAK;USA/indicator/SP.URB.TOTL.IN.ZS?format=json&amp;date=2014:2025&amp;per_page=100>); values after [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} continue the trend from [2015]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} to [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. |
| [Non-income]{.keep-word} drift: price gap, taste and availability. Drawn between a fall of [2]{.claim data-claim="proxy_united_states_2025_retail_dollar_decline_value"} percent a year, the US change in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, and growth of [17.8]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2022_adoption_drift_high"} percent a year, India's [job-board]{.keep-word} vacancy growth from [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} to [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Source: GFI US data; AltProtein.Jobs vacancy table. |
| [Animal-source]{.keep-word} protein supply: size of the diet that substitutes could replace. [19.67]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2023_animal_protein_supply_value"} grams per person per day in India and [31.52]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2023_animal_protein_supply_value"} in Pakistan; Pakistan's adjustment uses this ratio raised to a weight drawn between none and full. Source: [FAOSTAT Food Balances](<https://www.fao.org/faostat/en/#data/FBS>), seven [animal-source]{.keep-word} items. |
| Industry activity: Pakistan's thinner company base. Pakistan's activity per person relative to India's, drawn between [0.10]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2025_ecosystem_factor_low"} (directory rows) and [0.67]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2025_ecosystem_factor_high"} (vacancies in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}). Source: AltProtein.Jobs directory and vacancy table. |
| Price premium: enters through the drift only. GFI reports that most US [plant-based]{.keep-word} categories cost more than double their conventional counterparts. No comparable premium is observed for India or Pakistan, so none is entered. Source: GFI US retail market data. |

The calculation draws the four uncertain parameters independently from uniform ranges [10000]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2022_monte_carlo_draws_value"} times with a fixed seed. The tables report the tenth, fiftieth and ninetieth percentiles of the results as lower, middle and upper scenarios.

### The scenarios grow quickly from a very small base

| Country, year | Lower | Middle | Upper | Per person | US share |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| India, [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} | [69]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2026_market_value_p10"} | [93]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2026_market_value_p50"} | [125]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2026_market_value_p90"} | [0.06]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2026_per_capita_value_p50"} | [0.27]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2026_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p50"} |
| India, [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} | [102]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_market_value_p10"} | [188]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_market_value_p50"} | [334]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_market_value_p90"} | [0.12]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_per_capita_value_p50"} | [0.53]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p50"} |
| India, [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} | [166]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_p10"} | [448]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_p50"} | [1142]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_p90"} | [0.28]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_per_capita_value_p50"} | [1.22]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p50"} |
| Pakistan, [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} | [1.5]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2026_market_value_p10"} | [3.6]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2026_market_value_p50"} | [6.6]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2026_market_value_p90"} | [0.015]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2026_per_capita_value_p50"} | [0.06]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2026_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p50"} |
| Pakistan, [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} | [2.3]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2030_market_value_p10"} | [6.0]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2030_market_value_p50"} | [13.7]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2030_market_value_p90"} | [0.023]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2030_per_capita_value_p50"} | [0.10]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2030_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p50"} |
| Pakistan, [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} | [3.6]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_p10"} | [11.8]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_p50"} | [36.1]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_p90"} | [0.040]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_per_capita_value_p50"} | [0.17]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p50"} |

Lower, middle and upper values are market totals in million US dollars at constant [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} prices. Per person is the middle value per person in dollars. US share is that value as a percentage of US retail spending per person in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. India's middle scenario rises from [93]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2026_market_value_p50"} million dollars in [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} to [448]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_p50"} million in [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Even its upper [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} scenario leaves spending per person at [3.12]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_per_capita_share_of_us_2025_p90"} percent of the US level in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. Pakistan's middle scenario rises from [3.6]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2026_market_value_p50"} million dollars to [11.8]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_p50"} million over the same years. Pakistan has no domestic market observation, so its values rest entirely on the India anchor and the four relative proxies.

The comparison with the US uses observed US retail spending in [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}. The US section's model forecasts for [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} were not available for this edition. When they arrive, the same [per-person]{.keep-word} ratio can be computed against each forecast year.

### The range sits below GFI India's own scenarios

GFI India's [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} domestic scenarios of [7475]{.claim data-claim="proxyin_gfi_india_domestic_2030_low"}, [12779]{.claim data-claim="proxyin_gfi_india_domestic_2030_central"} and [18963]{.claim data-claim="proxyin_gfi_india_domestic_2030_high"} crore rupees convert, on the same assumptions, to [964]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_gfi_india_domestic_scenario_low"}, [1649]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_gfi_india_domestic_scenario_central"} and [2447]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_gfi_india_domestic_scenario_high"} million dollars. The share of this section's draws below GFI's lowest scenario is [100]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_draws_below_gfi_scenario_low"} percent. The two approaches differ in growth rate. From the [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} base, GFI's lowest scenario implies growth of [47]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_gfi_india_implied_growth_low"} percent a year and its central scenario [57]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_gfi_india_implied_growth_central"} percent. The fastest of this section's draws grows [34]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2030_fastest_draw_growth_max"} percent a year. GFI does not publish its model inputs, so the difference cannot yet be attributed to a single assumption. The report records the disagreement rather than choosing between the two. The IndexBox growth bands remain held for the reason given above.

The drift and the income elasticity move India's result most. With the drift fixed at its lower bound, India's middle [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} value is [127]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_drift_fixed_low"} million dollars; at its upper bound it is [1398]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_drift_fixed_high"} million. Fixing the elasticity at its bounds gives [332]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_elasticity_fixed_low"} and [622]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_elasticity_fixed_high"} million. For Pakistan, the [industry-activity]{.keep-word} factor matters most after the drift: its bounds give [3.5]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_ecosystem_fixed_low"} and [22.6]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_ecosystem_fixed_high"} million dollars, against [3.7]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_drift_fixed_low"} and [40.1]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_drift_fixed_high"} million for the drift.

Vacancy growth may include income growth; a sensitivity that subtracts the income contribution from the high drift bound gives an India [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} middle value of [215]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_market_value_drift_overlap_adjusted_p50"} million dollars. Pakistan's four multipliers may also overlap; removing its relative urban share and animal protein factors gives a [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} range of [2.6]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_without_urban_animal_p10"} to [25.3]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_market_value_without_urban_animal_p90"} million dollars.

### Projecting the drivers forward

The table carries the report's five drivers and two market proxies forward. Each projection is a speculative direction, not an estimate. The signposts name observations that would show which path is unfolding.

| Driver, projection and signpost |
| --- |
| Income. Evidence: Real income per person in [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} is projected at [2.17]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2035_income_index_from_2022_value"} times its [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} level in India and [1.25]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2035_income_index_from_2022_value"} times in Pakistan. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Buyers remain [high-income]{.keep-word} urban households in both countries. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: India's faster income growth widens the gap with Pakistan. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Income alone leaves both far below US spending per person. Signpost: IMF growth revisions; [processed-food]{.keep-word} spending in the next HCES and HIES rounds. |
| Affordability and price premium. Evidence: [40.4]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_india_unaffordable"} percent in India and [60.3]{.claim data-claim="nutrition_2024_pakistan_unaffordable"} percent in Pakistan could not afford a healthy diet in [2024]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}; most US [plant-based]{.keep-word} categories cost more than double their conventional counterparts. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Premium pricing limits purchase to occasional trial. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Volume growth depends on a price per serving near chicken or paneer. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: [Mass-market]{.keep-word} use requires prices competitive with pulses and eggs, not only with meat. Signpost: Price per serving at named retailers against chicken, dal and milk; SOFI affordability series. |
| Dietary practice. Evidence: [Animal-source]{.keep-word} protein supply of [19.67]{.claim data-claim="proxy_india_2023_animal_protein_supply_value"} grams per person per day in India and [31.52]{.claim data-claim="proxy_pakistan_2023_animal_protein_supply_value"} in Pakistan. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Indian sales lean on [plant-based]{.keep-word} dairy, which GFI India put at [250]{.claim data-claim="proxy_gfi_india_2022_plant_based_dairy"} of [351]{.claim data-claim="proxyin_gfi_india_domestic_2022_central"} crore rupees in [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}; Pakistani demand would centre on chicken and milk substitutes. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Products that fit existing meals gain over analogues of foods people already avoid. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: A larger [animal-food]{.keep-word} baseline gives Pakistan more to substitute, if products reach it. Signpost: Category split of retail sales; FAOSTAT supply trends; [meal-based]{.keep-word} choice studies. |
| Social legitimacy. Evidence: The culture matrix records variation without a national willingness score. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Acceptance is studied in purposive samples only. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: [Repeat-purchase]{.keep-word} evidence decides whether trial becomes habit. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Legitimacy may differ by community within each country more than between them. Signpost: A comparable bilateral survey with purchase and repeat questions. |
| Certification. Evidence: Halal conditions for cultivated meat are conditional and [product-specific]{.keep-word}. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: [Plant-based]{.keep-word} products need ordinary halal assurance; no cultivated product is on sale in either country to certify. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: A first certified cultivated product in a [Muslim-majority]{.keep-word} market would set a precedent. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Certification routes for [fermentation-derived]{.keep-word} ingredients become routine or remain contested. Signpost: Pakistan Halal Authority or Punjab Food Authority decisions on named products. |
| Public policy. Evidence: India records BioE3 support and FSSAI rules; Pakistan records halal institutions and a Punjab [novel-food]{.keep-word} provision. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: [Plant-based]{.keep-word} foods sell under existing rules; no cultivated product is authorised in either country. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: [Fermentation-derived]{.keep-word} foods may reach Indian shelves first. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Cultivated meat stays outside this estimate unless an authorisation occurs. Signpost: FSSAI [non-specified]{.keep-word} food approvals; Pakistani [novel-food]{.keep-word} notifications. |
| Industry activity. Evidence: India recorded [381]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_india_2023"} and [529]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_india_2025"} vacancies in [2023]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"} and [2025]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}; Pakistan recorded [33]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_pakistan_2023"} and [58]{.claim data-claim="jobs_vacancies_pakistan_2025"}. [2026]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: India has a local base of brands and ingredient makers; Pakistan has [4]{.claim data-claim="companies_register_pakistan_count"} register records and [2]{.claim data-claim="jobs_directory_pakistan_rows"} directory rows. [2030]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Indian hiring growth near its recent pace supports the upper scenarios. [2035]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}: Pakistan's path depends on a first local manufacturer. Signpost: AltProtein.Jobs annual vacancy counts; local product launches in Pakistan. |

### What would change these numbers

A second domestic observation would matter more than any refinement of the proxies. A verified retail sales total for India after [2022]{.allow-number data-number-kind="year"}, or any sales total for Pakistan, would replace an assumed path with an observed one. The team also needs to confirm the four definitions above, because the US forecasts will be stated in their own unit. Until then, these scenarios show the scale and direction implied by stated assumptions, and the companion data record every input with its source and locator.

## Published consumer studies leave the bilateral comparison open

::: {data-package="P07"}

The survey extraction contains [9]{.claim data-claim="package_p07_study_records"} study records and [29]{.claim data-claim="package_p07_finding_records"} findings. These studies differ in country, product, recruitment, wording and outcome. They are not all studies of India and Pakistan. The extraction contains a Pakistan sample descriptor but no India finding, so its row count cannot be presented as a bilateral consumer dataset.

Sample size describes the reported study or analytical group, not the representativeness of its recruitment. A large online convenience sample can still omit people without internet access or people unwilling to answer in the questionnaire's language. A national label in a paper therefore needs the actual sampling description before the result is interpreted as national demand.

The report retains the studies separately in a [country-and-measure]{.keep-word} table. It does not pool percentages, add overlapping respondents or estimate a common acceptance rate. A descriptor, a willingness category and a hypothetical choice result have different denominators. Apparent agreement between them does not create a comparable outcome.
